Canadian rapper and singer [[Drake (musician)|Drake]] has released 189 [[Single (music)|single]]s (including 106 as a featured artist) and six [[promotional single]]s. His music has been released on record labels [[Universal Motown Records]] and [[Republic Records]], along with subsidiaries [[Young Money Entertainment]], [[Cash Money Records]] and [[OVO Sound]]. With 170 million records sold worldwide,<ref>{{Cite news|date=December 12, 2019|title=Ed Sheeran, Drake and Justin Bieber: What were they doing 10 years ago?|language=en-GB|work=BBC News|url=https://www.bbc.com/news/newsbeat-50743503|access-date=January 6, 2022}}</ref> he is among the [[List of best-selling music artists|best-selling music artists in history]]. Drake has achieved thirteen number-one hits on the [[Billboard Hot 100|''Billboard'' Hot 100]].<ref name="auto">{{Cite magazine|title=Drake|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/drake|access-date=September 14, 2021|magazine=Billboard|archive-date=December 4, 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171204112239/https://www.billboard.com/music/drake|url-status=live}}</ref> ''Billboard'' hailed him as the "Artist of the 2010s Decade"<ref>{{Cite web|date=May 23, 2021|title=Drake Accepts Artist of the Decade Award With Son Adonis at 2021 Billboard Music Awards|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/awards/drake-artist-of-the-decade-award-2021-billboard-music-awards-9577019|access-date=September 14, 2021|website=www.billboard.com|language=en|archive-date=September 14, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210914163449/https://www.billboard.com/amp/articles/news/awards/9577019/drake-artist-of-the-decade-award-2021-billboard-music-awards|url-status=live}}</ref> and the 16th Greatest Artist of all time.<ref name="auto"/> RIAA ranks him as the top-selling digital artist of all time with 350 million in the United States.<ref>{{Cite web|title=Gold & Platinum|url=https://www.riaa.com/gold-platinum/|access-date=September 14, 2021|website=RIAA|language=en-US|archive-date=February 12, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180212231947/https://www.riaa.com/gold-platinum/|url-status=live}}</ref> He has the most number one singles on both the US [[Hot Rap Songs]] chart (with thirty) and the US [[Hot R&B/Hip-Hop Songs]] chart (also with thirty).<ref>{{cite magazine |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/drake-tops-jay-z-for-rbhip-hop-chart-no-1s-record-480496/ |title=Drake Tops Jay-Z For R&B/Hip-Hop Chart No. 1s Record |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |publisher=[[Prometheus Global Media]] |date=August 10, 2012 |access-date=August 11, 2012 |author=Pietroluongo, Silvio}}</ref>

Following him signing to Young Money Entertainment imprint, Drake's mixtape, ''[[So Far Gone (mixtape)|So Far Gone]]'' was repackaged as a [[So Far Gone (EP)|2009 release of his seven-song extended play]], containing three singles: "[[Best I Ever Had]]", "[[Successful (song)|Successful]]", and "[[I'm Goin' In]]". These singles peaked at numbers 2, 17, and 40 on the US [[Billboard Hot 100|''Billboard'' Hot 100]], respectively.<ref name="US-singles" /> In June 2010, Drake released his debut studio album, ''[[Thank Me Later]]'', which featured the single, "[[Find Your Love]]", peaking at number 5 on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100.

In November 2011, Drake released his second studio album, ''[[Take Care (album)|Take Care]]'', which produced singles such as "[[Marvins Room]]", "[[Headlines (Drake song)|Headlines]]", "[[Make Me Proud]]", "[[The Motto (Drake song)|The Motto]]", "[[Take Care (song)|Take Care]]", "[[HYFR (Hell Ya Fucking Right)]]", "[[Crew Love]]", and "Lord Knows", four of which reached the top 15 of the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 and were certified platinum by the RIAA (RIAA).<ref name="RIAA">{{cite certification|region=United States|title=|artist=Drake|type=single|access-date=February 14, 2026}}</ref><ref name="US-singles" /> "Take Care" became the most commercially successful single from the album in many overseas territories such as Australia, where it was certified double platinum by the [[Australian Recording Industry Association]] (ARIA), Ireland and the United Kingdom.<ref name="AUS">Peak chart positions in Australia:

 |url-status      = live
 }}</ref> In September 2013, Drake released his third studio album, ''[[Nothing Was the Same]]'', which produced two of his top 10 singles "[[Started from the Bottom]]" and "[[Hold On, We're Going Home]]".

Drake's fourth studio album ''[[Views (album)|Views]]'' was released in April 2016, and included the singles "[[Hotline Bling]]", "[[One Dance]]", "[[Pop Style]]", "[[Controlla]]", and "[[Too Good]]", all of which peaked within the top 20 on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 chart. "One Dance" became the most successful single of Drake's career, becoming an international hit and peaking at number one in fifteen countries, including Canada, the US, and the UK, where it became his first number-one single. "One Dance" has since also become the third [[List of most streamed songs on Spotify|most played song]] on [[streaming media]] service [[Spotify]], with over two billion individual streams.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.guinnessworldrecords.com/world-records/400103-most-streamed-track-on-spotify|title=Most streamed track on Spotify|access-date=July 21, 2020|archive-date=July 21, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200721055019/https://www.guinnessworldrecords.com/world-records/400103-most-streamed-track-on-spotify|url-status=live}}</ref>

Drake's fifth studio album, ''[[Scorpion (Drake album)|Scorpion]]'', was released in June 2018; all 25 tracks on the album entered the ''Billboard'' Hot 100. The album was supported by six singles, including the number-one singles "[[God's Plan (song)|God's Plan]]", "[[Nice for What]]", and "[[In My Feelings]]". In August 2018, Drake was featured on [[Travis Scott]]'s single, "[[Sicko Mode]]", which reached number one on the Hot 100 four months later, but he was not credited as a featured artist. He released his seventh mixtape ''[[Dark Lane Demo Tapes]]'', in May 2020, which spawned his seventh Hot 100 number-one single, "[[Toosie Slide]]". In March 2021, he released the extended play ''[[Scary Hours 2]]'', which included his eighth Hot 100 chart-topper, "[[What's Next (Drake song)|What's Next]]". In September 2021, Drake released his sixth studio album, ''[[Certified Lover Boy]]'', in which he got his ninth number-one single on the Hot 100 with "[[Way 2 Sexy]]", which features [[Future (rapper)|Future]] and [[Young Thug]]. In May 2022, he was featured alongside [[Tems]] on Future's single, "[[Wait for U]]", which became his tenth number-one single. In June 2022, Drake released his seventh studio album, ''[[Honestly, Nevermind]]'', from which he achieved his eleventh number-one single on the Hot 100 with "[[Jimmy Cooks]]", which features [[21 Savage]]. In October 2023, Drake released his eighth studio album, ''[[For All the Dogs]]'', from which he earned his twelfth and thirteenth number-one singles on the Hot 100 with "[[Slime You Out]]" and  "[[First Person Shooter (song)|First Person Shooter]]", which feature [[SZA]] and [[J. Cole]], respectively.
